12. using System;
13. using System.Collections.Generic;
14. using System.Text;
15. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Background;
16. using Windows.Devices.Bluetooth;
17. using Windows.Devices.Bluetooth.Advertisement;
18. using Windows.Devices.Bluetooth.Background;
19. using Windows.Storage;
20. using Windows.Storage.Streams;
21. 
22. namespace SDKTemplate
23. {
24.     // An out-of-process background task always implements the IBackgroundTask interface.
25.     // An in-process background task does not need to implement the IBackgroundTask interface,
26.     // but we do it here to make it easier to convert the task between in-process and out-of-process.
27.     // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
28.     public sealed class AdvertisementWatcherTask : IBackgroundTask
29.     {
30.         private IBackgroundTaskInstance backgroundTaskInstance;
31. 
32.         /// <summary>
33.         /// The entry point of a background task.
34.         /// </summary>
35.         /// <param name="taskInstance">The current background task instance.</param>
36.         public void Run(IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
37.         {
38.             backgroundTaskInstance = taskInstance;
39. 
40.             var details = (BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcherTriggerDetails)taskInstance.TriggerDetails;
41. 
42.             // In this example, the background task simply constructs a message communicated
43.             // to the App. For more interesting applications, a notification can be sent from here instead.
44.             StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder();
45. 
46.             // If the background watcher stopped unexpectedly, an error will be available here.
47.             var error = details.Error;
48.             if (error != BluetoothError.Success)
49.             {
50.                 builder.Append($"Error: {error}, ");
51.             }
52. 
53.             // The Advertisements property is a list of all advertisement events received
54.             // since the last task triggered. The list of advertisements here might be valid even if
55.             // the Error status is not Success since advertisements are stored until this task is triggered
56.             IReadOnlyList<BluetoothLEAdvertisementReceivedEventArgs> advertisements = details.Advertisements;
57.             builder.Append($"EventCount: {advertisements.Count}, ");
58. 
59.             // The signal strength filter configuration of the trigger is returned such that further
60.             // processing can be performed here using these values if necessary. They are read-only here.
61.             var rssiFilter = details.SignalStrengthFilter;
62. 
63.             builder.Append($"HighDBm: {FormatOptionalInt16(rssiFilter.InRangeThresholdInDBm)}, ");
64.             builder.Append($"LowDBm: {FormatOptionalInt16(rssiFilter.OutOfRangeThresholdInDBm)}, ");
65.             builder.Append($"Timeout (ms): {FormatOptionalTimeSpanMs(rssiFilter.OutOfRangeTimeout)}, ");
66.             builder.Append($"Sampling (ms): {FormatOptionalTimeSpanMs(rssiFilter.SamplingInterval)}");
67. 
68.             // Advertisements can contain multiple events that were aggregated, each represented by
69.             // a BluetoothLEAdvertisementReceivedEventArgs object.
70.             foreach (var advertisementEventArgs in advertisements)
71.             {
72.                 builder.Append($"\n[{advertisementEventArgs.Timestamp:T}] [{advertisementEventArgs.AdvertisementType}]: Rssi={advertisementEventArgs.RawSignalStrengthInDBm} dBm");
73.                 if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(advertisementEventArgs.Advertisement.LocalName))
74.                 {
75.                     builder.Append($", localName={advertisementEventArgs.Advertisement.LocalName}");
76.                 }
77. 
78.                 // Check if there are any manufacturer-specific sections.
79.                 // If there is, print the raw data of the first manufacturer section (if there are multiple).
80.                 var manufacturerData = advertisementEventArgs.Advertisement.ManufacturerData;
81.                 if (manufacturerData.Count > 0)
82.                 {
83.                     // Print the first one of the list
84.                     builder.Append($", manufacturerData=[{Utilities.FormatManufacturerData(manufacturerData[0])}]");
85.                 }
86.             }
87. 
88.             // Store the message in a local settings indexed by this task's name so that the foreground App
89.             // can display this message.
90.             ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings.Values[taskInstance.Task.Name] = builder.ToString();
91.         }
92. 
93.         private static string FormatOptionalInt16(short? number)
94.         {
95.             return number.HasValue ? number.Value.ToString() : "none";
96.         }
97. 
98.         private static string FormatOptionalTimeSpanMs(TimeSpan? span)
99.         {
100.             return span.HasValue ? span.Value.TotalMilliseconds.ToString() : "none";
101.         }
102. 
103.     }
104. 
105.     // An out-of-process background task always implements the IBackgroundTask interface.
106.     // An in-process background task does not need to implement the IBackgroundTask interface,
107.     // but we do it here to make it easier to convert the task between in-process and out-of-process.
108.     // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
109.     public sealed class AdvertisementPublisherTask : IBackgroundTask
110.     {
111.         private IBackgroundTaskInstance backgroundTaskInstance;
112. 
113.         /// <summary>
114.         /// The entry point of a background task.
115.         /// </summary>
116.         /// <param name="taskInstance">The current background task instance.</param>
117.         public void Run(IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
118.         {
119.             backgroundTaskInstance = taskInstance;
120. 
121.             var details = (BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisherTriggerDetails)taskInstance.TriggerDetails;
122. 
123.             // In this example, the background task simply constructs a message communicated
124.             // to the App. For more interesting applications, a notification can be sent from here instead.
125.             StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder();
126. 
127.             // If the background publisher stopped unexpectedly, an error will be available here.
128.             BluetoothError error = details.Error;
129.             if (error != BluetoothError.Success)
130.             {
131.                 builder.Append($"Error: {error}, ");
132.             }
133. 
134.             // The status of the publisher is useful to determine whether the advertisement payload is being serviced
135.             // It is possible for a publisher to stay in a Waiting state while radio resources are in use.
136.             BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisherStatus status = details.Status;
137.             builder.Append($"Publisher status: {status}");
138. 
139.             // Store the message in a local settings indexed by this task's name so that the foreground App
140.             // can display this message.
141.             ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings.Values[taskInstance.Task.Name] = builder.ToString();
142.         }
143.     }
144. }

31.         {

33.             {
34.                 Title = "Foreground watcher",

55.     public class Scenario

58.         public Type ClassType { get; set; }
59.     }
60. 
61.     sealed partial class App : Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application
62.     {
63.         /// <summary>
64.         /// Override the Application.OnBackgroundActivated method to handle background activation in
65.         /// the main process. This entry point is used when BackgroundTaskBuilder.TaskEntryPoint is
66.         /// not set during background task registration.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="args">Object that describes the background task being activated.</param>
69.         protected override void OnBackgroundActivated(BackgroundActivatedEventArgs args)
70.         {
71.             // Use the args.TaskInstance.Task.Name and/or args.TaskInstance.InstanceId to determine
72.             // which background task to run. In our case, the Name is sufficient.
73.             IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance = args.TaskInstance;
74.             string name = taskInstance.Task.Name;
75. 
76.             if (name == nameof(AdvertisementWatcherTask))
77.             {
78.                 new AdvertisementWatcherTask().Run(taskInstance);
79.             }
80.             else if (name == nameof(AdvertisementPublisherTask))
81.             {
82.                 new AdvertisementPublisherTask().Run(taskInstance);
83.             }
84.         }
85.     }
86. 
87.     static class FeatureDetection
88.     {
89.         // Reports whether the extended advertising and scanning features are supported:
90.         //
91.         // BluetoothAdapter.IsLowEnergyUncoded2MPhySupported property
92.         // BluetoothAdapter.IsLowEnergyCodedPhySupported property
93.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementReceivedEventArgs.PrimaryPhy property
94.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementReceivedEventArgs.SecondaryPhy property
95.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisher.PrimaryPhy property
96.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisher.SecondaryPhy property
97.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisherTrigger.PrimaryPhy property
98.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisherTrigger.SecondaryPhy property
99.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementScanParameters class
100.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher.UseUncoded1MPhy property
101.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher.UseCodedPhy property
102.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher.ScanParameters property
103.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher.UseHardwareFilter property
104.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcherTrigger.UseUncoded1MPhy property
105.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcherTrigger.UseCodedPhy property
106.         // BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcherTrigger.ScanParameters property
107.         // GattServiceProvider.UpdateAdvertisingParameters method
108.         // GattServiceProviderConnection.UpdateAdvertisingParameters method
109.         // GattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters.UseLowEnergyUncoded1MPhyAsSecondaryPhy property
110.         // GattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters.UseLowEnergyUncoded2MPhyAsSecondaryPhy property
111.         //
112.         // All of these features are available as a group, so testing one of them is sufficient to
113.         // check for the presence of all.
114.         public static bool AreExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParametersSupported => LazyAreExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParametersSupported.Value;
115. 
116.         private static Lazy<bool> LazyAreExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParametersSupported = new Lazy<bool>(DetectExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParameters);
117. 
118.         // Declare a dummy version of IGattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters3 because all we need is the UUID.
119.         [ComImport]
120.         [System.Runtime.InteropServices.Guid("A23546B2-B216-5929-9055-F1313DD53E2A")]
121.         [InterfaceType(ComInterfaceType.InterfaceIsIInspectable)]
122.         private interface IGattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters3
123.         {
124.         };
125.         private static bool DetectExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParameters()
126.         {
127.             // We will use GattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters.UseLowEnergyUncoded1MPhyAsSecondaryPhy
128.             // to detect this feature group.
129.             bool isPresentInMetadata = ApiInformation.IsPropertyPresent(
130.                 typeof(GattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters).FullName,
131.                 nameof(GattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters.UseLowEnergyUncoded1MPhyAsSecondaryPhy));
132.             if (!isPresentInMetadata)
133.             {
134.                 return false;
135.             }
136. 
137.             // The feature is present in metadata. See if it is available at runtime.
138.             // During the initial rollout of the feature, it may be unavailable at runtime
139.             // despite being declared in metadata.
140.             return (object)(new GattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters()) is IGattServiceProviderAdvertisingParameters3;
141.         }
142.     }
143. 
144.     static class Utilities
145.     {
146.         public static int? Int32FromBuffer(IBuffer buffer)
147.         {
148.             CryptographicBuffer.CopyToByteArray(buffer, out byte[] data);
149.             if (data.Length != sizeof(int))
150.             {
151.                 return null;
152.             }
153. 
154.             return BitConverter.ToInt32(data, 0);
155.         }
156. 
157.         public static IBuffer BufferFromUInt16(UInt16 value)
158.         {
159.             return CryptographicBuffer.CreateFromByteArray(BitConverter.GetBytes(value));
160.         }
161. 
162.         public static IBuffer BufferFromInt32(int value)
163.         {
164.             return CryptographicBuffer.CreateFromByteArray(BitConverter.GetBytes(value));
165.         }
166. 
167.         public static string FormatManufacturerData(BluetoothLEManufacturerData manufacturerData)
168.         {
169.             // 0x####: zzzzzz
170.             // where #### is the company ID as a four-digit hex value, and
171.             // zzzzzz is the manufacturer data as a hex-encoded byte string
172.             return $"0x{manufacturerData.CompanyId:X4}: {CryptographicBuffer.EncodeToHexString(manufacturerData.Data)}";
173.         }
174.     }
175. }

12. using System;
13. using System.Text;
14. using Windows.Devices.Bluetooth;
15. using Windows.Devices.Bluetooth.Advertisement;

22. namespace SDKTemplate
23. {
24.     /// <summary>
25.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
26.     /// </summary>
27.     public sealed partial class Scenario1_Watcher : Page
28.     {
29.         // A pointer back to the main page is required to display status messages.
30.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
31. 
32.         // For pretty-printing timestamps.
33.         DateTimeFormatter formatter = new DateTimeFormatter("longtime");
34. 
35.         // The Bluetooth LE advertisement watcher class is used to control and customize Bluetooth LE scanning.
36.         private BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher watcher;
37.         bool isWatcherStarted = false;
38. 
39.         // Capability of the Bluetooth radio adapter.
40.         private bool supportsCodedPhy = false;
41.         private bool supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters = false;
42. 
43.         public Scenario1_Watcher()
44.         {
45.             InitializeComponent();
46. 
47.             // Create and initialize a new watcher instance.
48.             watcher = new BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher();
49. 
50.             // Begin of watcher configuration. Configure the advertisement filter to look for the data advertised by the publisher
51.             // in Scenario 2 or 4. You need to run Scenario 2 on another Windows platform within proximity of this one for Scenario 1 to
52.             // take effect. The APIs shown in this Scenario are designed to operate only if the App is in the foreground. For background
53.             // watcher operation, please refer to Scenario 3.
54. 
55.             // Please comment out this following section (watcher configuration) if you want to remove all filters. By not specifying
56.             // any filters, all advertisements received will be notified to the App through the event handler. You should comment out the following
57.             // section if you do not have another Windows platform to run Scenario 2 alongside Scenario 1 or if you want to scan for
58.             // all LE advertisements around you.
59. 
60.             // For determining the filter restrictions programmatically across APIs, use the following properties:
61.             //      MinSamplingInterval, MaxSamplingInterval, MinOutOfRangeTimeout, MaxOutOfRangeTimeout
62. 
63.             // Part 1A: Configuring the advertisement filter to watch for a particular advertisement payload
64. 
65.             // First, let create a manufacturer data section we wanted to match for. These are the same as the one
66.             // created in Scenario 2 and 4.
67.             var manufacturerData = new BluetoothLEManufacturerData();
68. 
69.             // Then, set the company ID for the manufacturer data. Here we picked an unused value: 0xFFFE
70.             manufacturerData.CompanyId = 0xFFFE;
71. 
72.             // Finally set the data payload within the manufacturer-specific section
73.             // Here, use a 16-bit UUID: 0x1234 -> {0x34, 0x12} (little-endian)
74.             UInt16 uuidData = 0x1234;
75. 
76.             // Make sure that the buffer length can fit within an advertisement payload. Otherwise you will get an exception.
77.             manufacturerData.Data = Utilities.BufferFromUInt16(uuidData);
78. 
79.             // Add the manufacturer data to the advertisement filter on the watcher:
80.             watcher.AdvertisementFilter.Advertisement.ManufacturerData.Add(manufacturerData);
81. 
82.             // Part 1B: Configuring the signal strength filter for proximity scenarios
83. 
84.             // Configure the signal strength filter to only propagate events when in-range
85.             // Please adjust these values if you cannot receive any advertisement
86.             // Set the in-range threshold to -70dBm. This means advertisements with RSSI >= -70dBm
87.             // will start to be considered "in-range".
88.             watcher.SignalStrengthFilter.InRangeThresholdInDBm = -70;
89. 
90.             // Set the out-of-range threshold to -75dBm (give some buffer). Used in conjunction with OutOfRangeTimeout
91.             // to determine when an advertisement is no longer considered "in-range"
92.             watcher.SignalStrengthFilter.OutOfRangeThresholdInDBm = -75;
93. 
94.             // Set the out-of-range timeout to be 2 seconds. Used in conjunction with OutOfRangeThresholdInDBm
95.             // to determine when an advertisement is no longer considered "in-range"
96.             watcher.SignalStrengthFilter.OutOfRangeTimeout = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(2);
97. 
98.             // By default, the sampling interval is set to zero, which means there is no sampling and all
99.             // the advertisement received is returned in the Received event
100. 
101.             // End of watcher configuration. There is no need to comment out any code beyond this point.
102.         }
103. 
104.         /// <summary>
105.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
106.         /// We will enable/disable parts of the UI if the device doesn't support it.
107.         /// </summary>
108.         /// <param name="eventArgs">Event data that describes how this page was reached. The Parameter
109.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
110.         protected override async void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
111.         {
112.             // Attach a handler to process the received advertisement.
113.             // The watcher cannot be started without a Received handler attached
114.             watcher.Received += OnAdvertisementReceived;
115. 
116.             // Attach a handler to process watcher stopping due to various conditions,
117.             // such as the Bluetooth radio turning off or the Stop method was called
118.             watcher.Stopped += OnAdvertisementWatcherStopped;
119. 
120.             // Attach handlers for suspension to stop the watcher when the App is suspended.
121.             App.Current.Suspending += App_Suspending;
122.             App.Current.Resuming += App_Resuming;
123. 
124.             // Detect OS and default Bluetooth adapter support for features.
125.             if (FeatureDetection.AreExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParametersSupported)
126.             {
127.                 var adapter = await BluetoothAdapter.GetDefaultAsync();
128.                 if (adapter != null)
129.                 {
130.                     supportsCodedPhy = adapter.IsLowEnergyCodedPhySupported;
131.                     supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters = adapter.IsAdvertisementOffloadSupported;
132.                 }
133.                 if (!supportsCodedPhy)
134.                 {
135.                     Watcher1MAndCodedPhysReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by default Bluetooth adapter)";
136.                 }
137.                 if (!supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters)
138.                 {
139.                     WatcherPerformanceOptimizationsReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by default Bluetooth adapter)";
140.                 }
141.             }
142.             else
143.             {
144.                 Watcher1MAndCodedPhysReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by this version of Windows)";
145.                 WatcherPerformanceOptimizationsReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by this version of Windows)";
146. 
147.             }
148.             UpdateButtons();
149. 
150.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Press Run to start watcher.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
151.         }
152. 
153.         /// <summary>
154.         /// Invoked immediately before the Page is unloaded and is no longer the current source of a parent Frame.
155.         /// </summary>
156.         /// <param name="e">
157.         /// Event data that can be examined by overriding code. The event data is representative
158.         /// of the navigation that will unload the current Page unless canceled. The
159.         /// navigation can potentially be canceled by setting Cancel.
160.         /// </param>
161.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
162.         {
163.             // Remove local suspension handlers from the App since this page is no longer active.
164.             App.Current.Suspending -= App_Suspending;
165.             App.Current.Resuming -= App_Resuming;
166. 
167.             // Make sure to stop the watcher when leaving the context. Even if the watcher is not stopped,
168.             // scanning will be stopped automatically if the watcher is destroyed.
169.             watcher.Stop();
170. 
171.             // Always unregister the handlers to release the resources to prevent leaks.
172.             watcher.Received -= OnAdvertisementReceived;
173.             watcher.Stopped -= OnAdvertisementWatcherStopped;
174. 
175.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Navigating away. Watcher stopped.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
176.         }
177. 
178.         /// <summary>
179.         /// Invoked when application execution is being suspended.  Application state is saved
180.         /// without knowing whether the application will be terminated or resumed with the contents
181.         /// of memory still intact.
182.         /// </summary>
183.         /// <param name="sender">The source of the suspend request.</param>
184.         /// <param name="e">Details about the suspend request.</param>
185.         private void App_Suspending(object sender, Windows.ApplicationModel.SuspendingEventArgs e)
186.         {
187.             // Make sure to stop the watcher on suspend.
188.             watcher.Stop();
189.             isWatcherStarted = false;
190. 
191.             // Always unregister the handlers to release the resources to prevent leaks.
192.             watcher.Received -= OnAdvertisementReceived;
193.             watcher.Stopped -= OnAdvertisementWatcherStopped;
194. 
195.             rootPage.NotifyUser("App suspending. Watcher stopped.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
196.         }
197. 
198.         /// <summary>
199.         /// Invoked when application execution is being resumed.
200.         /// </summary>
201.         /// <param name="sender">The source of the resume request.</param>
202.         /// <param name="e"></param>
203.         private void App_Resuming(object sender, object e)
204.         {
205.             watcher.Received += OnAdvertisementReceived;
206.             watcher.Stopped += OnAdvertisementWatcherStopped;
207.             UpdateButtons();
208.         }
209. 
210.         /// <summary>
211.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Run button is pressed.
212.         /// </summary>
213.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
214.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
215.         private void RunButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
216.         {
217.             // By default, Windows will scan over the 1M PHYs.
218.             if (supportsCodedPhy)
219.             {
220.                 if (Watcher1MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsChecked.Value)
221.                 {
222.                     /// Enable the Bluetooth adapter to also scan over 2M and Coded PHYs.
223.                     watcher.UseCodedPhy = true;
224.                     // Required in order to specify multiple scan PHYs
225.                     watcher.AllowExtendedAdvertisements = true;
226.                 }
227.                 else
228.                 {
229.                     watcher.UseCodedPhy = false;
230.                     watcher.AllowExtendedAdvertisements = false;
231.                 }
232.             }
233. 
234.             if (supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters)
235.             {
236.                 if (WatcherPerformanceOptimizationsCheckBox.IsChecked.Value)
237.                 {
238.                     /// Enable the Bluetooth adapter to perform a scan with performance optimizations
239.                     /// by coexistence with other technologies, and offloading the filter to hardware.
240.                     /// The added feature could be use independently. For example, you can enable
241.                     /// hardware offload without enabling the performance optimization.
242.                     /// To enable hardware filter offload, you need to provide a filter pattern for matching.
243.                     /// For detailed instructions on setting up the filter,
244.                     /// refer to Part 1A and 1B in Scenario1_Watcher initialization.
245.                     watcher.ScanParameters = BluetoothLEAdvertisementScanParameters.CoexistenceOptimized();
246.                     watcher.UseHardwareFilter = true;
247.                 }
248.                 else
249.                 {
250.                     /// Disable performance optimizations and reset to default low latency.
251.                     watcher.UseHardwareFilter = false;
252.                     watcher.ScanParameters = BluetoothLEAdvertisementScanParameters.LowLatency();
253.                 }
254.             }
255. 
256.             // Calling watcher start will start the scanning if not already initiated by another client
257.             watcher.Start();
258.             isWatcherStarted = true;
259. 
260.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Watcher started.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
261.             UpdateButtons();
262.         }
263. 
264.         /// <summary>
265.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Stop button is pressed.
266.         /// </summary>
267.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
268.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
269.         private void StopButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
270.         {
271.             // Stopping the watcher will stop scanning if this is the only client requesting scan
272.             watcher.Stop();
273.             isWatcherStarted = false;
274.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Watcher stopped.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
275.             UpdateButtons();
276.         }
277. 
278.         /// <summary>
279.         /// Enable and disable buttons based on the watcher state and based on what
280.         /// features are supported.
281.         /// </summary>
282.         private void UpdateButtons()
283.         {
284.             Watcher1MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsEnabled = supportsCodedPhy && !isWatcherStarted;
285.             WatcherPerformanceOptimizationsCheckBox.IsEnabled = supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters && !isWatcherStarted;
286.             RunButton.IsEnabled = !isWatcherStarted;
287.             StopButton.IsEnabled = isWatcherStarted;
288.         }
289. 
290.         /// <summary>
291.         /// Invoked as an event handler when an advertisement is received.
292.         /// </summary>
293.         /// <param name="watcher">Instance of watcher that triggered the event.</param>
294.         /// <param name="e">Event data containing information about the advertisement event.</param>
295.         private async void OnAdvertisementReceived(BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher watcher, BluetoothLEAdvertisementReceivedEventArgs e)
296.         {
297.             // We can obtain various information about the advertisement we just received by accessing
298.             // the properties of the BluetoothLEAdvertisementReceivedEventArgs class
299. 
300.             var builder = new StringBuilder();
301. 
302.             // The timestamps of the event
303.             builder.Append($"[{formatter.Format(e.Timestamp)}]: ");
304. 
305.             // The type of advertisement
306.             builder.Append($"type={e.AdvertisementType}, ");
307. 
308.             // The received signal strength indicator (RSSI)
309.             builder.Append($"rssi={e.RawSignalStrengthInDBm} dBm");
310. 
311.             // The local name of the advertising device contained within the payload, if any
312.             string localName = e.Advertisement.LocalName;
313.             if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(localName))
314.             {
315.                 builder.Append($", name={localName}");
316.             }
317. 
318.             // Check if there are any manufacturer-specific sections.
319.             // If there is, print the raw data of the first manufacturer section (if there are multiple).
320.             var manufacturerData = e.Advertisement.ManufacturerData;
321.             if (manufacturerData.Count > 0)
322.             {
323.                 // Only print the first one of the list
324.                 builder.Append($", manufacturerData=[{Utilities.FormatManufacturerData(manufacturerData[0])}]");
325.             }
326. 
327.             // Serialize UI update to the main UI thread
328.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
329.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
330.             {
331.                 // Add another entry to the list.
332.                 ReceivedAdvertisementListBox.Items.Add(builder.ToString());
333.             });
334.         }
335. 
336.         /// <summary>
337.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the watcher is stopped or aborted.
338.         /// </summary>
339.         /// <param name="watcher">Instance of watcher that triggered the event.</param>
340.         /// <param name="eventArgs">Event data containing information about why the watcher stopped or aborted.</param>
341.         private void OnAdvertisementWatcherStopped(BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcher watcher, BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcherStoppedEventArgs eventArgs)
342.         {
343.             // Notify the user that the watcher was stopped
344.             rootPage.NotifyUser(string.Format("Watcher stopped or aborted: {0}", eventArgs.Error.ToString()), NotifyType.StatusMessage);
345.         }
346.     }
347. }

22.     /// <summary>
23.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     public sealed partial class Scenario2_Publisher : Page
26.     {
27.         // A pointer back to the main page is required to display status messages.
28.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
29. 
30.         // The Bluetooth LE advertisement publisher class is used to control and customize Bluetooth LE advertising.
31.         private BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisher publisher;
32.         bool isPublisherStarted = false;
33. 
34.         // Capability of the Bluetooth radio adapter.
35.         private bool supportsAdvertisingInDifferentPhy = false;
36. 
37.         public Scenario2_Publisher()
38.         {
39.             InitializeComponent();
40. 
41.             // Create and initialize a new publisher instance.
42.             publisher = new BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisher();
43. 
44.             // We need to add some payload to the advertisement. A publisher without any payload
45.             // or with invalid ones cannot be started. We only need to configure the payload once
46.             // for any publisher.
47. 
48.             // Add a manufacturer-specific section:
49.             // First, let create a manufacturer data section
50.             var manufacturerData = new BluetoothLEManufacturerData();
51. 
52.             // Then, set the company ID for the manufacturer data. Here we picked an unused value: 0xFFFE
53.             manufacturerData.CompanyId = 0xFFFE;
54. 
55.             // Finally set the data payload within the manufacturer-specific section
56.             // Here, use a 16-bit UUID: 0x1234 -> {0x34, 0x12} (little-endian)
57.             UInt16 uuidData = 0x1234;
58. 
59.             // Make sure that the buffer length can fit within an advertisement payload. Otherwise you will get an exception.
60.             manufacturerData.Data = Utilities.BufferFromUInt16(uuidData);
61. 
62.             // Add the manufacturer data to the advertisement publisher:
63.             publisher.Advertisement.ManufacturerData.Add(manufacturerData);
64. 
65.             // Display the information about the published payload
66.             PublisherPayloadBlock.Text = $"Published payload information: {Utilities.FormatManufacturerData(manufacturerData)}";
67. 
68.             // Display the current status of the publisher
69.             PublisherStatusBlock.Text = $"Published Status: {publisher.Status}";
70.         }
71. 
72.         /// <summary>
73.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
74.         ///
75.         /// We will enable/disable parts of the UI if the device doesn't support it.
76.         /// </summary>
77.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached. The Parameter
78.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
79.         protected override async void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
80.         {
81.             // Attach a event handler to monitor the status of the publisher, which
82.             // can tell us whether the advertising has been serviced or is waiting to be serviced
83.             // due to lack of resources. It will also inform us of unexpected errors such as the Bluetooth
84.             // radio being turned off by the user.
85.             publisher.StatusChanged += OnPublisherStatusChanged;
86. 
87.             // Attach handlers for suspension to stop the publisher when the App is suspended.
88.             App.Current.Suspending += App_Suspending;
89.             App.Current.Resuming += App_Resuming;
90. 
91.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Press Run to start publisher.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
92. 
93.             // Determine whether the default Bluetooth adapter supports 2M and Coded PHY.
94.             if (FeatureDetection.AreExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParametersSupported)
95.             {
96.                 var adapter = await BluetoothAdapter.GetDefaultAsync();
97.                 if (adapter != null)
98.                 {
99.                     supportsAdvertisingInDifferentPhy = adapter.IsLowEnergyUncoded2MPhySupported && adapter.IsLowEnergyCodedPhySupported;
100.                 }
101.                 if (!supportsAdvertisingInDifferentPhy)
102.                 {
103.                     Publisher2MAndCodedPhysReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by default Bluetooth adapter)";
104.                 }
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                     Publisher2MAndCodedPhysReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by this version of Windows)";
109.             }
110. 
111.             UpdateButtons();
112.         }
113. 
114.         /// <summary>
115.         /// Invoked immediately after the Page is unloaded and is no longer the current source of a parent Frame.
116.         /// </summary>
117.         /// <param name="e">
118.         /// Event data that can be examined by overriding code. The event data is representative
119.         /// of the navigation that has unloaded the current Page.
120.         /// </param>
121.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
122.         {
123.             // Remove local suspension handlers from the App since this page is no longer active.
124.             App.Current.Suspending -= App_Suspending;
125.             App.Current.Resuming -= App_Resuming;
126. 
127.             // Make sure to stop the publisher when leaving the context. Even if the publisher is not stopped,
128.             // advertising will be stopped automatically if the publisher is destroyed.
129.             publisher.Stop();
130. 
131.             // Always unregister the handlers to release the resources to prevent leaks.
132.             publisher.StatusChanged -= OnPublisherStatusChanged;
133.         }
134. 
135.         /// <summary>
136.         /// Invoked when application execution is being suspended.  Application state is saved
137.         /// without knowing whether the application will be terminated or resumed with the contents
138.         /// of memory still intact.
139.         /// </summary>
140.         /// <param name="sender">Unused</param>
141.         /// <param name="e">Details about the suspend request.</param>
142.         private void App_Suspending(object sender, Windows.ApplicationModel.SuspendingEventArgs e)
143.         {
144.             // Make sure to stop the publisher on suspend.
145.             publisher.Stop();
146.             isPublisherStarted = false;
147. 
148.             // Always unregister the handlers to release the resources to prevent leaks.
149.             publisher.StatusChanged -= OnPublisherStatusChanged;
150. 
151.             rootPage.NotifyUser("App suspending. Publisher stopped.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
152.         }
153. 
154.         /// <summary>
155.         /// Invoked when application execution is being resumed.
156.         /// </summary>
157.         /// <param name="sender">The source of the resume request.</param>
158.         /// <param name="e"></param>
159.         private void App_Resuming(object sender, object e)
160.         {
161.             publisher.StatusChanged += OnPublisherStatusChanged;
162.             UpdateButtons();
163.         }
164. 
165.         /// <summary>
166.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Run button is pressed.
167.         /// </summary>
168.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
169.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
170.         private void RunButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
171.         {
172.             // By default, the BT radio uses the 1M PHY primary/1M PHY secondary configuration,
173.             // which matches the Windows default configuration.
174.             // If both coded and 2M PHYs are supported, use the preferred configuration.
175.             if (supportsAdvertisingInDifferentPhy)
176.             {
177.                 if (Publisher2MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsChecked.Value)
178.                 {
179.                     // Enable the Bluetooth adapter to only advertise over 2M and Coded PHYs.
180.                     publisher.PrimaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.CodedPhy;
181.                     publisher.SecondaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.Uncoded2MPhy;
182.                     publisher.UseExtendedAdvertisement = true;
183.                 }
184.                 else
185.                 {
186.                     // Disable the Bluetooth adapter to advertise over 2M and Coded PHYs and reset it back to 1M PHYs only.
187.                     publisher.PrimaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.Uncoded1MPhy;
188.                     publisher.SecondaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.Uncoded1MPhy;
189.                     publisher.UseExtendedAdvertisement = false;
190.                 }
191.             }
192. 
193.             // Calling publisher start will start the advertising if resources are available to do so.
194.             publisher.Start();
195.             isPublisherStarted = true;
196. 
197.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Publisher started.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
198.             UpdateButtons();
199.         }
200. 
201.         /// <summary>
202.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Stop button is pressed.
203.         /// </summary>
204.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
205.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
206.         private void StopButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
207.         {
208.             // Stopping the publisher will stop advertising the published payload
209.             publisher.Stop();
210.             isPublisherStarted = false;
211. 
212.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Publisher stopped.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
213.             UpdateButtons();
214.         }
215. 
216.         /// <summary>
217.         /// Enable and disable buttons based on the publisher state and based on what
218.         /// features are supported.
219.         /// </summary>
220.         private void UpdateButtons()
221.         {
222.             Publisher2MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsEnabled = supportsAdvertisingInDifferentPhy && !isPublisherStarted;
223.             RunButton.IsEnabled = !isPublisherStarted;
224.             StopButton.IsEnabled = isPublisherStarted;
225.         }
226. 
227.         /// <summary>
228.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the status of the publisher changes.
229.         /// </summary>
230.         /// <param name="publisher">Instance of publisher that triggered the event.</param>
231.         /// <param name="eventArgs">Event data containing information about the publisher status change event.</param>
232.         private async void OnPublisherStatusChanged(
233.             BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisher publisher,
234.             BluetoothLEAdvertisementPublisherStatusChangedEventArgs e)
235.         {
236.             // This event handler can be used to monitor the status of the publisher.
237.             // We can catch errors if the publisher is aborted by the system
238. 
239.             // Update the publisher status displayed in the sample
240.             // Serialize UI update to the main UI thread
241.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
242.             await Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
243.             {
244.                 PublisherStatusBlock.Text = $"Published Status: {e.Status}, Error: {e.Error}";
245.             });
246.         }
247.     }
248. }

23. namespace SDKTemplate
24. {
25.     /// <summary>
26.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
27.     /// </summary>
28.     public sealed partial class Scenario3_BackgroundWatcher : Page
29.     {
30.         // A pointer back to the main page is required to display status messages.
31.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
32. 
33.         // The background task registration for the background advertisement watcher
34.         private IBackgroundTaskRegistration taskRegistration = null;
35. 
36.         // The watcherTrigger used to configure the background task registration
37.         private BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcherTrigger watcherTrigger;
38. 
39.         // Capabilities of the Bluetooth radio adapter.
40.         private bool supportsCodedPhy = false;
41.         private bool supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters = false;
42. 
43.         // A name is given to the task in order for it to be identifiable as the background task for this scenario.
44.         private static readonly string taskName = nameof(AdvertisementWatcherTask);
45. 
46.         const int E_DEVICE_NOT_AVAILABLE = unchecked((int)0x800710df); // HRESULT_FROM_WIN32(ERROR_DEVICE_NOT_AVAILABLE)
47. 
48.         public Scenario3_BackgroundWatcher()
49.         {
50.             InitializeComponent();
51. 
52.             // Create and initialize a new watcherTrigger to configure it.
53.             watcherTrigger = new BluetoothLEAdvertisementWatcherTrigger();
54. 
55.             // Configure the advertisement filter to look for the data advertised by the publisher in Scenario 2 or 4.
56.             // You need to run Scenario 2 on another Windows platform within proximity of this one for Scenario 3 to
57.             // take effect.
58. 
59.             // Unlike the APIs in Scenario 1 which operate in the foreground. This API allows the developer to register a background
60.             // task to process advertisement packets in the background. It has more restrictions on valid filter configuration.
61.             // For example, exactly one single matching filter condition is allowed (no more or less) and the sampling interval
62. 
63.             // For determining the filter restrictions programmatically across APIs, use the following properties:
64.             //      MinSamplingInterval, MaxSamplingInterval, MinOutOfRangeTimeout, MaxOutOfRangeTimeout
65. 
66.             // Part 1A: Configuring the advertisement filter to watch for a particular advertisement payload
67. 
68.             // First, let create a manufacturer data section we wanted to match for. These are the same as the one
69.             // created in Scenario 2 and 4. Note that in the background only a single filter pattern is allowed per watcherTrigger.
70.             var manufacturerData = new BluetoothLEManufacturerData();
71. 
72.             // Then, set the company ID for the manufacturer data. Here we picked an unused value: 0xFFFE
73.             manufacturerData.CompanyId = 0xFFFE;
74. 
75.             // Finally set the data payload within the manufacturer-specific section
76.             // Here, use a 16-bit UUID: 0x1234 -> {0x34, 0x12} (little-endian)
77.             UInt16 uuidData = 0x1234;
78. 
79.             // Make sure that the buffer length can fit within an advertisement payload. Otherwise you will get an exception.
80.             manufacturerData.Data = CryptographicBuffer.CreateFromByteArray(BitConverter.GetBytes(uuidData));
81. 
82.             // Add the manufacturer data to the advertisement filter on the watcherTrigger:
83.             watcherTrigger.AdvertisementFilter.Advertisement.ManufacturerData.Add(manufacturerData);
84. 
85.             // Part 1B: Configuring the signal strength filter for proximity scenarios
86. 
87.             // Configure the signal strength filter to only propagate events when in-range
88.             // Please adjust these values if you cannot receive any advertisement
89.             // Set the in-range threshold to -70dBm. This means advertisements with RSSI >= -70dBm
90.             // will start to be considered "in-range".
91.             watcherTrigger.SignalStrengthFilter.InRangeThresholdInDBm = -70;
92. 
93.             // Set the out-of-range threshold to -75dBm (give some buffer). Used in conjunction with OutOfRangeTimeout
94.             // to determine when an advertisement is no longer considered "in-range"
95.             watcherTrigger.SignalStrengthFilter.OutOfRangeThresholdInDBm = -75;
96. 
97.             // Set the out-of-range timeout to be 2 seconds. Used in conjunction with OutOfRangeThresholdInDBm
98.             // to determine when an advertisement is no longer considered "in-range"
99.             watcherTrigger.SignalStrengthFilter.OutOfRangeTimeout = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(2000);
100. 
101.             // By default, the sampling interval is set to be disabled, or the maximum sampling interval supported.
102.             // The sampling interval set to MaxSamplingInterval indicates that the event will only watcherTrigger once after it comes into range.
103.             // Here, set the sampling period to 1 second, which is the minimum supported for background.
104.             watcherTrigger.SignalStrengthFilter.SamplingInterval = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(1000);
105.         }
106. 
107.         /// <summary>
108.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
109.         ///
110.         /// We will enable/disable parts of the UI if the device doesn't support it.
111.         /// </summary>
112.         /// <param name="eventArgs">Event data that describes how this page was reached. The Parameter
113.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
114.         protected override async void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
115.         {
116.             // If there is an existing task registration, subscribe to its completion event.
117.             FindTaskRegistration();
118.             if (taskRegistration != null)
119.             {
120.                 taskRegistration.Completed += OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
121.             }
122.             UpdateButtons();
123. 
124.             // Attach handlers for suspension to disconnect from the background task when the App is suspended.
125.             App.Current.Suspending += App_Suspending;
126.             App.Current.Resuming += App_Resuming;
127. 
128.             // Check whether the local Bluetooth adapter supports the Coded PHYs and hardware off load.
129.             if (FeatureDetection.AreExtendedAdvertisingPhysAndScanParametersSupported)
130.             {
131.                 BluetoothAdapter adapter = await BluetoothAdapter.GetDefaultAsync();
132. 
133.                 if (adapter != null)
134.                 {
135.                     supportsCodedPhy = adapter.IsLowEnergyCodedPhySupported;
136.                     supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters = adapter.IsAdvertisementOffloadSupported;
137.                 }
138.                 if (!supportsCodedPhy)
139.                 {
140.                     WatcherTrigger1MAndCodedPhysReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by default Bluetooth adapter)";
141.                 }
142.                 if (!supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters)
143.                 {
144.                     WatcherTriggerPerformanceOptimizationsReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by default Bluetooth adapter)";
145.                 }
146.             }
147.             else
148.             {
149.                 WatcherTrigger1MAndCodedPhysReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by this version of Windows)";
150.                 WatcherTriggerPerformanceOptimizationsReasonRun.Text = "(Not supported by this version of Windows)";
151.             }
152. 
153.             UpdateButtons();
154.         }
155. 
156.         /// <summary>
157.         /// Invoked immediately after the Page is unloaded and is no longer the current source of a parent Frame.
158.         /// </summary>
159.         /// <param name="e">
160.         /// Event data that can be examined by overriding code. The event data is representative
161.         /// of the navigation that has unloaded the current Page.
162.         /// </param>
163.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
164.         {
165.             // Remove local suspension handlers from the App since this page is no longer active.
166.             App.Current.Suspending -= App_Suspending;
167.             App.Current.Resuming -= App_Resuming;
168. 
169.             // Since the watcher is registered in the background, the background task will be triggered when the App is closed
170.             // or in the background. To unregister the task, press the Stop button.
171.             if (taskRegistration != null)
172.             {
173.                 // Always unregister the handlers to release the resources to prevent leaks.
174.                 taskRegistration.Completed -= OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
175.             }
176.         }
177. 
178.         /// <summary>
179.         /// Invoked when application execution is being suspended.  Application state is saved
180.         /// without knowing whether the application will be terminated or resumed with the contents
181.         /// of memory still intact.
182.         /// </summary>
183.         /// <param name="sender">Unused.</param>
184.         /// <param name="e">Details about the suspend request.</param>
185.         private void App_Suspending(object sender, SuspendingEventArgs e)
186.         {
187.             if (taskRegistration != null)
188.             {
189.                 // Always unregister the handlers to release the resources to prevent leaks.
190.                 taskRegistration.Completed -= OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
191.             }
192.             rootPage.NotifyUser("App suspending.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
193.         }
194. 
195.         /// <summary>
196.         /// Invoked when application execution is being resumed.
197.         /// </summary>
198.         /// <param name="sender">The source of the resume request.</param>
199.         /// <param name="e"></param>
200.         private void App_Resuming(object sender, object e)
201.         {
202.             // If there is an existing task registration, subscribe to its completion event.
203.             // (We unsubscribed at suspension.)
204.             FindTaskRegistration();
205.             if (taskRegistration != null)
206.             {
207.                 taskRegistration.Completed += OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
208.             }
209.             UpdateButtons();
210.         }
211. 
212.         /// <summary>
213.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Run button is pressed.
214.         /// </summary>
215.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
216.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
217.         private async void RunButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
218.         {
219.             // Applications registering for background watcherTrigger must request permission.
220.             BackgroundAccessStatus backgroundAccessStatus = await BackgroundExecutionManager.RequestAccessAsync();
221.             // Here, we do not fail the registration even if the access is not granted. Instead, we allow
222.             // the watcherTrigger to be registered and when the access is granted for the Application at a later time,
223.             // the watcherTrigger will automatically start working again.
224. 
225.             // At this point we assume we haven't found any existing tasks matching the one we want to register
226.             // First, configure the watcherTrigger.
227. 
228.             // Default Windows will scan over the 1M PHYs.
229.             if (supportsCodedPhy)
230.             {
231.                 if (WatcherTrigger1MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsChecked.Value)
232.                 {
233.                     // Enable the Bluetooth adapter to also scan over 2M and Coded PHYs.
234.                     watcherTrigger.UseCodedPhy = true;
235.                     // Required in order to specify multiple scan PHYs
236.                     watcherTrigger.AllowExtendedAdvertisements = true;
237.                 }
238.                 else
239.                 {
240.                     // Disable scanning over 2M and Coded PHYs.
241.                     watcherTrigger.UseCodedPhy = false;
242.                     watcherTrigger.AllowExtendedAdvertisements = false;
243.                 }
244.             }
245. 
246.             if (supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters)
247.             {
248.                 if (WatcherTriggerPerformanceOptimizationsCheckBox.IsChecked.Value)
249.                 {
250.                     // Enable the Bluetooth adapter to perform a background scan with performance optimizations.
251.                     watcherTrigger.ScanParameters = BluetoothLEAdvertisementScanParameters.CoexistenceOptimized();
252.                 }
253.                 else
254.                 {
255.                     // Disable scanning with performance optimizations and reset it to default low latency.
256.                     watcherTrigger.ScanParameters = BluetoothLEAdvertisementScanParameters.LowLatency();
257.                 }
258.             }
259. 
260.             // Create a background task builder with the watcherTrigger and name.
261.             // Omitting the task entry point results in an in-process background task.
262.             // See App.OnBackgroundActivated for the in-process background task entry point.
263.             // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
264.             var builder = new BackgroundTaskBuilder();
265.             builder.SetTrigger(watcherTrigger);
266.             builder.Name = taskName;
267. 
268.             // Now perform the registration. This may throw if the system does not have a Bluetooth radio.
269.             try
270.             {
271.                 taskRegistration = builder.Register();
272.             }
273.             catch (Exception ex) when (ex.HResult == E_DEVICE_NOT_AVAILABLE)
274.             {
275.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Cannot register background task. Maybe the system does not have a Bluetooth radio.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
276.             }
277. 
278.             if (taskRegistration != null)
279.             {
280.                 // For this scenario, attach an event handler to display the result processed from the background task
281.                 taskRegistration.Completed += OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
282. 
283.                 // Even though the watcherTrigger is registered successfully, it might be blocked. Notify the user if that is the case.
284.                 if ((backgroundAccessStatus == BackgroundAccessStatus.AlwaysAllowed) || (backgroundAccessStatus == BackgroundAccessStatus.AllowedSubjectToSystemPolicy))
285.                 {
286.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background watcher registered.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
287.                 }
288.                 else
289.                 {
290.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background tasks may be disabled for this app", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
291.                 }
292.             }
293.             UpdateButtons();
294.         }
295. 
296.         /// <summary>
297.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Stop button is pressed.
298.         /// </summary>
299.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
300.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
301.         private void StopButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
302.         {
303.             // Unregistering the background task will stop scanning if this is the only client requesting scan
304.             taskRegistration.Unregister(true);
305.             taskRegistration = null;
306.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Background watcher unregistered.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
307. 
308.             UpdateButtons();
309.         }
310. 
311.         /// <summary>
312.         /// If we have not already found a task registration, try to find it.
313.         /// </summary>
314.         private void FindTaskRegistration()
315.         {
316.             if (taskRegistration == null)
317.             {
318.                 // Look among the already-registered tasks for the one with the matching name.
319.                 foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks.Values)
320.                 {
321.                     if (task.Name == taskName)
322.                     {
323.                         taskRegistration = task;
324.                         break;
325.                     }
326.                 }
327.             }
328.         }
329. 
330.         /// <summary>
331.         /// Enable and disable buttons based on the task registration state and based on what
332.         /// features are supported.
333.         /// </summary>
334.         private void UpdateButtons()
335.         {
336.             bool isRegistered = taskRegistration != null;
337.             WatcherTrigger1MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsEnabled = supportsCodedPhy && !isRegistered;
338.             WatcherTriggerPerformanceOptimizationsCheckBox.IsEnabled = supportsHardwareOffloadedFilters && !isRegistered;
339.             RunButton.IsEnabled = !isRegistered;
340.             StopButton.IsEnabled = isRegistered;
341.         }
342. 
343.         /// <summary>
344.         /// Handle background task completion.
345.         /// </summary>
346.         /// <param name="task">The task that is reporting completion.</param>
347.         /// <param name="e">Arguments of the completion report.</param>
348.         private async void OnBackgroundTaskCompleted(BackgroundTaskRegistration task, BackgroundTaskCompletedEventArgs e)
349.         {
350.             // We get the advertisement(s) processed by the background task
351.             if (ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings.Values.TryGetValue(taskName, out object backgroundMessage))
352.             {
353.                 // Serialize UI update to the main UI thread
354.                 // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
355.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
356.                 {
357.                     // Display these information on the list
358.                     ReceivedAdvertisementListBox.Items.Add(backgroundMessage);
359.                 });
360.             }
361.         }
362.     }
363. }

157.                 // Always unregister the handlers to release the resources to prevent leaks.
158.                 taskRegistration.Completed -= OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
159.             }
160.         }
161. 
162.         /// <summary>
163.         /// Invoked when application execution is being resumed.
164.         /// </summary>
165.         /// <param name="sender">The source of the resume request.</param>
166.         /// <param name="e"></param>
167.         private void App_Resuming(object sender, object e)
168.         {
169.             // If there is an existing task registration, subscribe to its completion event.
170.             // (We unsubscribed at suspension.)
171.             FindTaskRegistration();
172.             if (taskRegistration != null)
173.             {
174.                 taskRegistration.Completed += OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
175.             }
176.             UpdateButtons();
177.         }
178. 
179.         /// <summary>
180.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Run button is pressed.
181.         /// </summary>
182.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
183.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
184.         private async void RunButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
185.         {
186.             // Applications registering for background publisherTrigger must request for permission.
187.             BackgroundAccessStatus backgroundAccessStatus = await BackgroundExecutionManager.RequestAccessAsync();
188.             // Here, we do not fail the registration even if the access is not granted. Instead, we allow
189.             // the publisherTrigger to be registered and when the access is granted for the Application at a later time,
190.             // the publisherTrigger will automatically start working again.
191. 
192.             // First, configure the publisherTrigger.
193.             if (supportsAdvertisingInDifferentPhy)
194.             {
195.                 // By default, the BT radio uses the 1M PHY primary/1M PHY secondary configuration,
196.                 // which matches the Windows default configuration.
197.                 // If both coded and 2M PHYs are supported. Use the preferred configuration.
198.                 if (PublisherTrigger2MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsChecked.Value)
199.                 {
200.                     publisherTrigger.UseExtendedFormat = true;
201.                     publisherTrigger.PrimaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.CodedPhy;
202.                     publisherTrigger.SecondaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.Uncoded2MPhy;
203.                 }
204.                 else
205.                 {
206.                     publisherTrigger.UseExtendedFormat = false;
207.                     publisherTrigger.PrimaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.Uncoded1MPhy;
208.                     publisherTrigger.SecondaryPhy = BluetoothLEAdvertisementPhyType.Uncoded1MPhy;
209.                 }
210.             }
211. 
212.             // Create a background task builder with the watcherTrigger and name.
213.             // Omitting the task entry point results in an in-process background task.
214.             // See App.OnBackgroundActivated for the in-process background task entry point.
215.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
216.             var builder = new BackgroundTaskBuilder();
217.             builder.SetTrigger(publisherTrigger);
218.             builder.Name = taskName;
219. 
220.             // Now perform the registration. This may throw if the system does not have a Bluetooth radio.
221.             try
222.             {
223.                 taskRegistration = builder.Register();
224.             }
225.             catch (Exception ex) when (ex.HResult == E_DEVICE_NOT_AVAILABLE)
226.             {
227.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Cannot register background task. Maybe the system does not have a Bluetooth radio.", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
228.             }
229. 
230.             if (taskRegistration != null)
231.             {
232.                 // For this scenario, attach an event handler to display the result processed from the background task
233.                 taskRegistration.Completed += OnBackgroundTaskCompleted;
234. 
235.                 // Even though the publisherTrigger is registered successfully, it might be blocked. Notify the user if that is the case.
236.                 if ((backgroundAccessStatus == BackgroundAccessStatus.AlwaysAllowed) || (backgroundAccessStatus == BackgroundAccessStatus.AllowedSubjectToSystemPolicy))
237.                 {
238.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background publisher registered.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
239.                 }
240.                 else
241.                 {
242.                     rootPage.NotifyUser("Background tasks may be disabled for this app", NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
243.                 }
244.             }
245.             UpdateButtons();
246.         }
247. 
248.         /// <summary>
249.         /// Invoked as an event handler when the Stop button is pressed.
250.         /// </summary>
251.         /// <param name="sender">Instance that triggered the event.</param>
252.         /// <param name="e">Event data describing the conditions that led to the event.</param>
253.         private void StopButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
254.         {
255.             // Unregistering the background task will stop advertising if this is the only client requesting
256.             taskRegistration.Unregister(true);
257.             taskRegistration = null;
258.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Background publisher unregistered.", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
259. 
260.             UpdateButtons();
261.         }
262. 
263.         /// <summary>
264.         /// If we have not already found a task registration, try to find it.
265.         /// </summary>
266.         private void FindTaskRegistration()
267.         {
268.             if (taskRegistration == null)
269.             {
270.                 // Look among the already-registered tasks for the one with the matching name.
271.                 foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks.Values)
272.                 {
273.                     if (task.Name == taskName)
274.                     {
275.                         taskRegistration = task;
276.                         break;
277.                     }
278.                 }
279.             }
280.         }
281. 
282.         /// <summary>
283.         /// Enable and disable buttons based on the task registration state and based on what
284.         /// features are supported.
285.         /// </summary>
286.         private void UpdateButtons()
287.         {
288.             bool isRegistered = taskRegistration != null;
289.             PublisherTrigger2MAndCodedPhysCheckBox.IsEnabled = supportsAdvertisingInDifferentPhy && !isRegistered;
290.             RunButton.IsEnabled = !isRegistered;
291.             StopButton.IsEnabled = isRegistered;
292.         }
293. 
294.         /// <summary>
295.         /// Handle background task completion.
296.         /// </summary>
297.         /// <param name="task">The task that is reporting completion.</param>
298.         /// <param name="e">Arguments of the completion report.</param>
299.         private async void OnBackgroundTaskCompleted(BackgroundTaskRegistration task, BackgroundTaskCompletedEventArgs eventArgs)
300.         {
301.             // We get the status changed processed by the background task
302.             if (ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings.Values.TryGetValue(taskName, out object o) &&
303.                 o is string backgroundMessage)
304.             {
305.                 // Serialize UI update to the main UI thread
306.                 // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
307.                 await Dispatcher.RunAsync(Windows.UI.Core.CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
308.                 {
309.                     // Display the status change
310.                     PublisherStatusBlock.Text = backgroundMessage;
311.                 });
312.             }
313.         }
314.     }
315. }
316.

<a id="background-tasks"></a>
## Background Tasks`IBackgroundTask` / `BackgroundTaskRegistration` are not the recommended model. Use the WinAppSDK [`BackgroundTaskBuilder`](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder) (introduced in 1.7), or move the work to push-driven activation / Windows Task Scheduler. See the [background task migration strategy](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy).**If you keep the classic out-of-process model** (`Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.BackgroundTaskBuilder` with a string `TaskEntryPoint` such as `"Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask"` + `builder.Register()`) — which is the low-risk way to preserve existing behavior — then two things are **runtime prerequisites**, not optional:1. The app must be **packaged** (keep `Package.appxmanifest`), and the **build** manifest must declare an `<Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="<Namespace>.<TaskClass>">` for **every** `TaskEntryPoint` string used in code. This declaration lives in the UWP source manifest — carry it into the WinUI 3 project's root `Package.appxmanifest` (see the manifest checklist below). If it is missing, `builder.Register()` **throws at runtime** (`0x80070032`/class-not-registered) even though the app builds and launches fine.   ```xml
   <Application ...>
     <Extensions>
       <Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask">
         <BackgroundTasks>
           <Task Type="systemEvent" />
           <Task Type="timer" />
         </BackgroundTasks>
       </Extension>
     </Extensions>
   </Application>
   ```2. Wrap `Register()` in `try/catch` and surface the failure in the UI (e.g. the Status text). The SDK samples call `Register()` with no guard, so a missing-declaration failure otherwise presents as a **silent dead control** (the button click does nothing, Status never changes) — the hardest kind of regression to notice because there is no build error and no crash.
<exited with exit code 0>
--- RESULT ---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<exited with exit code 0>

48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)

61.             }

93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}

148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151.

52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62. 
63.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73. 
74.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81. 
82.             // Ensure the current window is active
83.             Window.Current.Activate();
84. 
85.             // Some samples want access to the LaunchActivatedEventArgs.
86.             LaunchCompleted(e);
87.         }
88. 
89.         private Frame CreateRootFrame()
90.         {
91.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
92. 
93.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
94.             // just ensure that the window is active
95.             if (rootFrame == null)
96.             {
97.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
98.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
99. 
100.                 // Set the default language
101.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
102.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
103. 
104.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
105.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
106.             }
107. 
108.             return rootFrame;
109.         }
110. 
111.         /// <summary>
112.         /// Invoked when Navigation to a certain page fails
113.         /// </summary>
114.         /// <param name="sender">The Frame which failed navigation</param>
115.         /// <param name="e">Details about the navigation failure</param>
116.         void OnNavigationFailed(object sender, NavigationFailedEventArgs e)
117.         {
118.             throw new Exception("Failed to load Page " + e.SourcePageType.FullName);
119.         }
120. 
121.         // Add any application constructor code in here.
122.         partial void Construct();
123. 
124.         // Add any OnLaunched customization here.
125.         partial void LaunchCompleted(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e);
126.     }
127. }
128.
